# Windows parity ledger

This ledger maps every macOS (Swift) test to the Rust test that covers the same
behavior in the Windows port. It is how the project keeps **zero drift** between
the two apps: CI (`.github/workflows/windows-parity.yml`) fails when a Swift test
is missing from this file, when a row names a Swift test that no longer exists,
or when a row marked `ported` points at a Rust test that is not there.

The specs in `docs/spec/` are the source of truth for behavior; the
**Behavior** column is a one-line summary taken from their test-parity
checklists (spec 01 §7, 02 §5, 03 §7, 04 §5), or from the test name where the
specs do not list the test.

## How to use this file

- **IDs.** Each Swift test has a stable ID, `<file>::<class>::<method>`.
  Swift Testing `@Test` functions count too (the class part is the enclosing
  type, or `global` at file scope). `make test-core` checks in
  `Tests/Manual/CoreTestsMain.swift` use the `assertTrue` label, lower-cased,
  as the method part; two labels that give the same ID fail CI. The IDs come
  from `windows/scripts/parity/extract_swift_tests.py`; run it to list them.
- **Status.** One of:
  - `todo`: not ported yet. CI accepts it.
  - `ported`: covered by the Rust test(s) in the **Rust test** column. CI checks
    that each one exists, is a test (`#[test]`, `#[tokio::test]`, a `#[test]`
    inside `proptest!`, ...), and is not `#[ignore]`d.
  - `n/a`: macOS-only, with no Windows equivalent. The **Notes** column must
    give the reason (CI checks that it is not empty).
- **Rust test.** `crates/<crate>/tests/<file>.rs::<fn>`, relative to `windows/`.
  Several tests may be listed, separated by `<br>`. A Rust test may cover more
  than one Swift test.
- **Workstream.** The workstream that owns the port (see
  `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`). Rows are grouped by workstream; the issue
  number is in each heading.
- **Same commit.** When you port a test, flip its row to `ported` in the same
  commit as the Rust test.
- **Changed Swift test.** When a change edits the body of a Swift test whose
  row is `ported`, the drift guard requires that row, or one of the Rust test
  files it names, to change in the same change: check the Rust test against
  the new Swift expectations and update whichever is out of date (comments and
  formatting in the Swift test don't count).
- **New Swift test.** When a macOS change adds, renames or deletes a test, CI
  lists the missing and stale IDs. Run
  `python3 windows/scripts/parity/check_parity.py --print-missing-rows` to get
  `todo` rows to paste into the right section, and delete stale rows (or
  rename them when the test was renamed).
- **Local check.** `python3 windows/scripts/parity/check_parity.py` prints the
  same summary CI writes to the job summary.

## Features without Swift tests

Behavior the specs require on Windows that no Swift test covers, either because
it is Windows-specific (a platform mapping from spec 01 §8 or spec 04 §2.7) or
because the specs call for a fix or an invariant the Swift suite does not
check. Each still needs a Rust test. The same status rules apply; CI checks
that `ported` rows name Rust tests that exist. Fuzz targets are tracked by the
workstream that owns each parser (spec 02 §6, spec 03 §8) and are not listed
here.

| ID | Feature | Spec | Workstream | Status | Rust test | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FEAT-core-net-set-cookie-separate` | Every `Set-Cookie` header is collected separately, and the cookie parser still accepts comma-folded values | 01 §1.1.3 | ws-core-net | ported | `crates/tokenmon-core/tests/test_net_cancel_cookies.rs::test_set_cookie_headers_collects_each_line`<br>`crates/tokenmon-core/tests/test_net_cancel_cookies.rs::test_split_folded_set_cookie_keeps_expires_commas` | |
| `FEAT-core-net-cancel-is-skipped` | A dropped or aborted request future maps to a `Cancelled` error that the poller classifies as skipped | 01 §8 | ws-core-net | ported | `crates/tokenmon-core/tests/test_net_cancel_cookies.rs::test_cancelled_request_is_cancelled_not_network_error` | |
| `FEAT-core-storage-atomic-write` | Atomic file write: temp file in the same folder, then replace (`MoveFileExW` REPLACE_EXISTING + WRITE_THROUGH); no staging file left behind on failure | 01 §1.3, §8 | ws-core-storage | ported | `crates/tokenmon-core/tests/storage_files.rs::test_write_atomic_replaces_existing_file`<br>`crates/tokenmon-core/tests/storage_files.rs::test_file_store_reports_failed_write` | |

## Divergences

Every intentional difference between the macOS app and the Windows port. A
change that adds a divergence must add a row here in the same commit.

### Fixed in Swift too (no longer divergent)

| ID | Area | What changed | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Grok gRPC parser (ws-grok) | `scanForField7Products` / `parseProductSubMessage` bound every varint read to the sub-message end. Spec 02 §4 item 1 asked Rust to "fix rather than copy"; upstream faulknerpearce/token_monitor#51 (commit 488be7c) fixed the Swift parser the same way, with regression tests (`testNestedKeyVarintPastMessageEndDoesNotCrash`, `testProductKeyVarintPastMessageEndDoesNotCrash`, `testProductValueVarintPastMessageEndRejectsProduct`). | Untrusted network input; the old Swift code trapped on `UInt64(end - index)`. Both apps now behave the same, and the Rust tests port the Swift ones. |
| D2 | Grok gRPC parser (ws-grok) | The product scan stops at depth 64 (`maxProductScanDepth`). Spec 02 §4 item 2; fixed in Swift by the same upstream #51, with `testDeeplyNestedMessagesDoNotOverflowStack`. | Adversarial nesting overflowed the stack. Rust uses the same cap so results match. |

### Intentional divergences

| ID | Area | macOS behavior | Windows behavior | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D3 | Grok product order (ws-grok) | Unknown products with equal display index sort in dictionary order (effectively nondeterministic). | Stable sort by (display index, id). | Spec 02 §1.2.5 and §4 item 3: deterministic output, testable. Only affects ties between unknown product ids. |
| D4 | Grok REST map parser (ws-grok) | Swift `as? Double` reads JSON `true`/`false` as 1/0. | Booleans are not numbers. | Spec 02 §1.2.7 and §4 item 4 allow it; a boolean is never a usage percent. |
| D5 | ChatGPT account-id fallback (ws-chatgpt) | When no known claim is present, the `account_id` substring scan iterates the JWT claims in unspecified dictionary order. | Document (JSON) order. | Spec 03 §2.4 and §6 item 2: deterministic. Same result whenever only one claim matches, which is the case the Swift test covers. |
| D6 | Numeric safety (all parsers) | Some Swift conversions trap or produce NaN on hostile input (e.g. `Int(f64)` on huge window seconds, `Double("nan")`). | Saturating conversions, NaN and infinity guarded, no panics. | Spec 02 §6 and spec 03 §8 fuzz invariants. Values that Swift handles without trapping give the same result. |
| DC1 | ChatGPT usage parse errors (ws-chatgpt) | A body that is not valid JSON makes `JSONSerialization` throw a raw `NSError`; valid JSON that is not an object gives `badResponse("Unexpected usage payload")`. | Every non-object body (invalid JSON, HTML, array, empty) is `BadResponse("Unexpected usage payload")`. | Spec 03 §2.5 says HTML too maps to this error; one typed error is easier to show and test. |
| DC2 | Redirects, schemes and cookie hosts (ws-secfix-a, #44) | `URLSession` follows redirects anywhere and `resolve` lets an absolute path replace the base. | `ProviderHttp` follows only `https` redirects to the request's own host (`new`) or the provider's `AuthConfig` hosts (`for_hosts`), refuses a plain `http` target other than loopback, drops `Set-Cookie` when the final host is not in that list, and refuses a `path` that leaves the base origin (`resolve` itself still resolves RFC 3986 for parity). | A redirect must not carry `Referer`/`Origin`/extra headers to another site, downgrade to http, or inject cookies into the stored session. Tests: `crates/tokenmon-core/tests/test_net_redirects.rs`. |
| DC2 | ChatGPT poller seams (ws-chatgpt) | The poller reads and writes `ChatGPTAuthSession` directly (`applyRefreshedCookies`, `needsSignIn`). | `ChatGptPoller` takes the `RollingCookieSession` trait (extends core `AuthSession` with `apply_refreshed_cookies` and `set_needs_sign_in`) and a boxed fetch function; the polling loop is driven by ws-core-polling through `refresh_now`. | Core `AuthSession` has no setter for those two operations; the real session implements the trait. |
| DU1 | `DayKey` parsing (ws-core-usage) | `startOfDay(for:)` builds the date leniently, so month 13 or day 40 roll over into a later date. | Out-of-range month or day gives `None`, like any other malformed key. | A rolled-over date would silently attribute usage to the wrong day. Keys come from disk. |
| DU2 | Bar count cap (ws-core-usage) | `buildDays` / `buildWeeklyWindowDays` allocate one bar per day of whatever period the server reports. | At most 3,700 bars (`MAX_BAR_DAYS`). | A corrupt reset date years away must not allocate millions of bars. |
| DU3 | JSON number strings (ws-core-usage) | `Double("0x1p3")` parses hexadecimal floats. | Hex float strings give `None`. | Not a format any provider emits; Rust's `f64::from_str` is stricter. |
| DU4 | Calendar (ws-core-usage) | Day maths use `Calendar.current` (host time zone). | Every function takes an injected `Calendar` (fixed offset or `Clock::local_offset_at`); nothing reads the host zone implicitly. | Deterministic tests and the spec's Clock contract. |
| D-UPD1 | Update source and assets (ws-update) | Feed is `releases/latest`; tag `v1.2.3`; picks `TokenMon*.zip`/`.pkg`; verifies codesign, bundle id, version. | Feed is the release list (a macOS `latest` release would hide a newer Windows one); only `windows-vX.Y.Z` tags and `tokenmon-windows-*` `.zip`/`.msi` assets; verification is SHA-256 plus PE structure (Authenticode and version-resource checks are TODO until signing exists). | The repo ships several platforms; signing is deferred (plan: Distribution). |
| DS1 | Settings decode (ws-settings) | A corrupt `UserDefaults` value is typed by the plist; each key is read independently. | The whole `settings.json` is one JSON object: a file that is not valid JSON (or has an out-of-range number literal such as `1e999`) decodes to all defaults, while valid JSON with a wrong-typed value falls back per key. Poll and threshold clamps also apply to floats and 64-bit extremes without trapping. | Spec 01 §2 and §8; corrupt files must never panic (fuzz target `tokenmon_core_settings_decode`). |
| DK1 | Digit figures (ws-ui-kit) | `bodyDigit` / `microDigit` use monospaced digits. | Slint cannot select tabular figures; the digit styles use the same size and semibold weight in the proportional font. | No Slint API for OpenType `tnum`; columns of digits can shift by a pixel as values change. |
| DK2 | Weekday labels (ws-ui-kit) | First two characters of the locale abbreviated weekday. | English only (`Mo`, `Tu`, ...). | Locale support comes with a localization pass; strings are produced by `kit::daily::weekday_label` so it is one place to change. |
| DK3 | Segmented bar widths (ws-ui-kit) | Computed from the live container width. | `kit::segments::layout_segments` computes widths for the track width the caller passes (372 px in a card); the component renders precomputed widths. | Slint expressions cannot sum over a model. The 420 px panel width is fixed, so the width is a constant. |
| D7 | Overview history decode (ws-overview) | Decodes whatever arrays the file holds; a malformed day key is kept as a string key. | Decode truncates each array to 24 hours, zeroes negative or non-finite weights and tokens, drops day keys that are not canonical `yyyy-MM-dd`, and rejects files over 4 MiB (all as an empty history). Unknown series keys still discard the whole file, as in Swift. | The file is untrusted input (fuzzed). Real files are unaffected: they hold 24 non-negative values and canonical keys. |
| D8 | Claude transcript cache (ws-claude) | Replacement of a transcript file is detected with the inode (`fileIdentifier`) plus size and mtime. | On Windows the file id is not read (std's `file_index` is unstable, no `unsafe`); detection relies on size, mtime and the tail check of D10. Unix builds use the inode. | Spec 03 §1.6.2 allows `None`. |
| D9 | Claude token counts (ws-claude) | `Int64(min(v, Double(Int64.max)))` traps when `v >= 2^63`. | Saturates at `i64::MAX`; all sums saturate. | Hostile transcripts must not crash the app (spec 03 §8). |
| D11 | Claude poller seams (ws-claude) | The poller reads and writes `ClaudeAuthSession` directly (`needsSignIn`), keeps `lastError` as a `String`, and picks its default code-usage loader by whether a fetch was injected. | `ClaudeUsagePoller` takes the `ClaudeSession` trait (core `AuthSession` plus `set_needs_sign_in`), keeps the typed `ProviderError` (the "Sign in to Claude to load usage." text becomes `NotSignedIn`), and always takes an explicit code-usage seam (`no_code_usage()` for hermetic tests, `live_code_usage()` in the app). | Core `AuthSession` has no setter for the sign-in flag; the panel formats typed errors; an explicit seam cannot silently read the real home directory. |
| D10 | Claude transcript append check (ws-claude) | An append is accepted when the byte before the saved offset is `\n`, so a same-size rewrite that ends in a newline reads as "no new bytes" and keeps stale totals. | The 64 bytes before the saved offset must still match what was parsed (and end in `\n`); otherwise the file is parsed again in full. | Found by the `claude_cache_refresh` fuzz target; fix rather than copy. Regression: `test_regression_same_size_rewrite_is_reparsed`. |
| DN1 | HTML sniffing (ws-core-net) | `looksLikeHTML` decodes the first 512 bytes as UTF-8; a multi-byte character cut by the 512-byte window fails the decode and the body is "not HTML". | A character cut by the window is ignored; only genuinely invalid UTF-8 means "not HTML". | An HTML page whose 512th byte lands inside a character must still be seen as HTML (bot-challenge and login detection). |
| DN2 | Retry-After HTTP dates (ws-core-net) | Parsed with a fixed `EEE, dd MMM yyyy HH:mm:ss zzz` formatter. | Parsed with chrono's RFC 2822 parser, which also accepts numeric offsets and other legal RFC 2822 spellings. | Superset of the Swift format; every date Swift accepts gives the same seconds. |
| OR1 | OpenRouter secrecy (ws-openrouter) | `Unexpected JSON: <localizedDescription>` may quote the offending value. | `Unexpected JSON (<category> error at line L, column C)`: no response values in error text. The API key is an `ApiKey` newtype (redacted `Debug`, no `Display`) and is scrubbed from transport errors. | A server that reflects the bearer key in a body must not get it into error strings or logs (issue #19). |
| OR2 | OpenRouter activity shares (ws-openrouter) | `cost / sum` can exceed 100 in total when costs overflow to infinity. | Costs are normalised by the largest before dividing, so shares sum to at most 100 and are never NaN. | Found by the `openrouter_activity` fuzz invariants; identical results for any realistic input. |
| D-UI1 | Settings window (ws-ui-settings) | Launch at Login can wait for approval in System Settings (caption plus "Open Login Items…"). Account subtitles truncate in the middle. Rows show the 16 px provider logo. | No approval state on Windows, so the caption and button are absent; a failed autostart write is shown as a red caption and the switch reverts. Subtitles elide at the end (Slint has no middle elide). Rows show an accent-colored initial until ws-ui-kit's logo assets land. | Windows autostart has no approval flow (Task Manager disabling is read as off). Slint limitation. Logo swap is a one-line change in `settings.slint`. |
| DG1 | Grok REST wrapper depth (ws-grok) | `parseAny` follows `usage`/`data`/... wrappers with no depth limit. | Stops after 32 levels (the JSON parser itself already stops at 128). | Spec 02 §1.2.7; no real response nests more than a few levels. Hostile payloads cannot burn stack or time. |
| DG2 | Grok response size (ws-grok) | `URLSession.data` buffers the whole body. | Bodies over 2 MiB fail as a network error. | The billing reply is a few hundred bytes; bounds memory against a hostile server. |
| DG3 | Grok duplicate products (ws-grok) | Duplicate ids across payloads add with `+=` on the struct field (no clamp, despite spec wording). | The sum is clamped to 100. | The model invariant is 0...100 everywhere; matches spec 02 §1.2.5. |
| DG4 | Grok cancellation (ws-grok) | A cancelled fetch throws `CancellationError`, which the poller maps to "skipped". | Dropping the fetch future is the cancellation; nothing is recorded. | Rust has no cooperative cancel error; same observable behavior. |
| DG5 | Grok history format (ws-grok) | SwiftData `history.store` imported once. | Not imported. The JSON file keeps the macOS shape (Apple reference-date doubles) so a copied file loads. | Spec 02 §1.4; no legacy store exists on Windows. |
| DW1 | Sign-in cookie capture (ws-webauth) | The ChatGPT-style preferred session cookie is matched by exact name only in the Windows `AuthConfig` contract. | A chunked-only NextAuth login (`<name>.0`, `<name>.1`, no bare `<name>`) also counts as the session; a blank session cookie never does. | `AuthConfig.preferred_session_cookie` is a string, the Swift policy is a closure. Chunked tokens are the reason the family exists (spec 01 1.6.5 table says "that family"). |
| DW2 | Sign-in cookie capture (ws-webauth) | Cookie names and values are copied into the `Cookie` header as read. | Cookies with control characters, a `;` in the value, or a non-token name are skipped. | Defense against header injection from a hostile provider page into the stored session. Real cookies are unaffected. |
| DW3 | Sign-in browser (ws-webauth) | WKWebView can navigate anywhere. | Only `http(s)` (and `about:blank` for popups) may load; permission prompts are denied, downloads cancelled, host objects, web messaging, DevTools and password autosave are off. | Provider pages are untrusted (spec brief security rules); none of these are needed to sign in. |
| DW4 | Sign-in popup wait (ws-webauth) | The 200 ms x 40 popup poll runs before the return delay. | The poll runs when the return delay expires (the only moment a popup can be open, since `onReturned` fires only with no popup open), then captures immediately. | Same observable behavior; makes the 8 s budget reachable and testable. |
| DW5 | Sign-in window chrome (ws-webauth) | SwiftUI header, spinners, icon buttons and tooltips. | Native Win32 controls with text buttons (`Back`, `Forward`, `Reload`/`Stop`, `Close popup`, `Finish Sign-In`); the footer spinner and address tooltip are omitted (status text says "Finishing sign-in..."). | A native chrome keeps provider pages in a separate window from app UI; no extra Slint/HTML surface to harden. |
| DW6 | Sign-in profile (ws-webauth) | One isolated WebKit data store per provider, cleared on sign-out. | One user-data folder per provider **per session**, deleted when the window closes and purged at startup. | Non-persistent by requirement: no browser profile outlives a sign-in. Sign-out still deletes any open window's cookies. |
| DC1 | Response size cap (ws-cursor, resolved by ws-auth-session) | Cursor `ProviderHTTP` calls have no body cap. | Core `ProviderHttp` reads incrementally and fails with `BadResponse("Response too large")` past 16 MiB (`DEFAULT_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`; `ProviderHttp::with_max_response_bytes` and `RequestOptions::max_response_bytes` override). The cap applies to every provider and to the decoded (post-gzip) size, and a declared `Content-Length` over the cap is refused before reading. | One shared transport; a hostile or broken endpoint cannot exhaust memory. Tests: `crates/tokenmon-core/tests/test_net_body_cap.rs`. #50: providers now pass tighter per-request caps (Claude and ChatGPT 512 KiB, OpenRouter 2 MiB, Cursor 4 MiB; each crate's `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`), and the Grok protobuf scan collects at most `MAX_SCAN_FIELDS` (100 000) fields. |
| DA1 | Session store write failures (ws-auth-session) | `save(cookieHeader:)` ignores a failed store write and stays signed in until restart. | `ProviderAuthSession::save`/`adopt` return `Result`; on a refused write the session stays signed out and `last_auth_error` carries the reason. | A session that cannot be persisted would vanish on restart with no explanation. |
| DA2 | Empty cookie header (ws-auth-session) | `cookieHeader()` returns `""` after a refresh deleted every essential cookie. | `cookie_header()` returns `None` for an empty stored value (the store still holds `""`). | Pollers treat empty as absent; one fewer state to handle. |
| DP1 | Network reachability (ws-power) | `NWPathMonitor` reports `satisfied`. | `NotifyNetworkConnectivityHintChange`: only level `InternetAccess` is satisfied; captive-portal (`ConstrainedInternetAccess`), local-only, hidden and unknown are not. Resume fallback (30 s) is unchanged. | Spec 01 section 1.7 allows any "internet reachable" signal. The hint API needs no COM apartment and is equivalent to the Network List Manager IPv4/IPv6 internet flags. A captive portal must not release polling early. |
| D-OV1 | Overview segment heights (ws-overview) | When thin-slice lifts to the minimum height exceed the tallest slice's headroom, Swift subtracts them from the tallest slice alone and it can go below the minimum (negative height). | The tallest slice still pays alone when it can; otherwise every slice above the minimum pays in proportion to its headroom, so no slice drops below the minimum. | Found by a proptest counterexample (commits 73385e1, 1b805fc, `test_overview_chart.rs`). The bar total is unchanged; only inputs where Swift produced a negative height differ. |

### Parity quirks preserved

Spec 03 §6 lists Swift behaviors that look odd. The Windows port **keeps** them
so both apps show the same numbers; changing any of them means changing the
Swift app as well (a `windows-parity` issue), not only the port.

| ID | Area | Quirk kept | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | ChatGPT usage (ws-chatgpt) | `primary_window: null` blocks the `five_hour` fallback (the fallback key is used only when the first key is absent); same for `secondary_window` / `weekly`. | Spec 03 §2.5, §6 item 1. Open question to the maintainer on #5 whether to fix it in both apps. |
| Q2 | OpenCode models (ws-opencode) | `modelUsage.sessionCount`: rows with an empty session id add 1; a row with an id overwrites the count with the number of distinct ids. | Spec 03 §3.6, §6 item 3. |
| Q3 | OpenCode monthly window (ws-opencode) | With no Go session, the monthly window anchors at `now` (monthly ≈ $0). | Spec 03 §6 item 4. |
| Q4 | OpenCode rolling window (ws-opencode) | The rolling window has no upper time bound. | Spec 03 §6 item 5. |
| Q5 | Claude transcripts (ws-claude) | Decoding is type-strict per declared field: a string token count drops the line. | Spec 03 §6 item 6. |
| Q6 | Claude transcripts (ws-claude) | Consuming an unterminated but valid JSON tail forces a full re-parse next time (correct, slower). | Spec 03 §6 item 7. |
| Q7 | Claude org id (ws-claude) | The `lastActiveOrg` cookie-name match is case-sensitive, while the allowlist is case-insensitive. | Spec 03 §6 item 8. |
| Q8 | OpenRouter pricing (ws-openrouter) | `:free` slugs are not price-normalised and stay $0. | Spec 03 §6 item 9. |
| Q9 | ChatGPT tests (ws-chatgpt) | `testAccountIDFromAccessToken` keeps the misspelt claim `chatggpt_account_id` to exercise the `account_id` substring fallback. | Spec 03 §6 item 10. Port the fixture as-is. |
| Q10 | ChatGPT usage booleans (ws-chatgpt) | `allowed` / `limit_reached` use `as? Bool`, which Foundation bridges from JSON numbers 0 and 1 (and `true`/`false`); any other value falls back to the default. | Copied exactly. Tests: `test_parse_root_fallback_and_bool_bridging`. |

### Platform mappings

macOS services replaced by a Windows equivalent with the same observable
behavior (spec 01 §8, spec 04 §2.7, plan Decisions). The tests for these are the
`n/a` rows above (no equivalent) or the `FEAT-` rows (new Windows behavior).

| ID | macOS | Windows | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Keychain, one generic-password vault item | DPAPI (user scope) vault file `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TokenMon\vault.bin` | Credential Manager rejected: 2,560-byte blob cap. Separate-item adoption dropped. |
| P2 | `~/Library/Application Support/TokenMon` (0700, files 0600) | `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TokenMon\` with an owner-only DACL | Same file names and formats. |
| P3 | `UserDefaults` | JSON settings file, same key names and types | |
| P4 | Menu-bar status item with per-segment click regions | One tray icon, tooltip, right-click menu per segment | Per-pixel hit-testing is impossible in the notification area. |
| P5 | `NSWorkspace` sleep/wake, `NWPathMonitor` | `WM_POWERBROADCAST`, Network List Manager | `SystemWakeGate` semantics unchanged. |
| P6 | `UNUserNotificationCenter` | WinRT toast with an AppUserModelID | No permission prompt. |
| P7 | `SMAppService` launch at login | HKCU `Run` key, `StartupApproved` check | |
| P8 | `WKWebView` non-persistent data store | WebView2 with a per-provider user-data folder | |
| P9 | `.app` zip/`.pkg` update, code signature, quarantine, `/bin/sh` relaunch | `tokenmon-windows-*` zip/`.msi` on `windows-v*` tags, Authenticode (signing deferred), Mark-of-the-Web, `--wait-for-pid` relaunch | |
| P10 | `LegacyCacheCleanup`, `ModelMonitor` folder move, SwiftData import | Not needed | Matching tests are `n/a`. |
| P11 | Dock show/hide (activation policy) | Not needed; single-instance guard added | |
